## Legend

| Field | Values |
|-------|--------|
| **Status** | `bug` wrong vs CE ground truth · `stub` method exists, does nothing · `partial` incomplete implementation · `missing` not started |
| **Severity** | `blocking` crashes or prevents play · `major` visible gameplay deviation · `minor` polish / edge-case |

Items marked ✅ in this document have been confirmed fixed since the last audit.
Items marked 🔶 are addressed on an unmerged branch.

| C1 | **Sniper perk uses d100 instead of d10.** FIXED 2026-06-02 — changed to `getRandomInt(1, 10) <= LUK`. CE ref: `combat.cc:3891 randomBetween(1, 10)`. | `src/combat/Combat.ts` | `combat.cc:3891–3897` | major | fixed |
| C2 | **Critical hit level formula uses uniform `/20` thresholds.** FIXED 2026-06-02 — uses CE's non-uniform breakpoints: ≤20→0, ≤45→1, ≤70→2, ≤90→3, ≤100→4, >100→5. Roll is `d100 + BetterCriticals`. CE ref: `combat.cc:4102 attackComputeCriticalHit()`. | `src/combat/Combat.ts` | `combat.cc:4102–4118` | major | fixed |
| C3 | **YAAM damage formula now matches CE.** FIXED 2026-06-02 — (a) `/2` halving step added after divisor; (b) DT subtracted BEFORE multiply (CE line 6795); (c) ammoRM adjusts DT not DR: `calcDT = DT - ammoRM`; if `calcDT < 0` → extra DR = `abs(calcDT) * 10`. All three divergences corrected. CE ref: `combat.cc:6767-6814 damageModCalculateYaam`. **⚠️ 2026-10-24 parity audit: contradicted — CE YAAM never adds damage; see `ce_audit/combat.md` COMBAT-13 / §33.** | `combat.ts` | `combat.cc:6767–6813 damageModCalculateYaam()` | minor | fixed |

CE ref: `combat.cc:4437-4440 attackDetermineToHit`. | `combat.ts` | `combat.cc:4440 attackDetermineToHit()` | minor | fixed |

The `TODO` comment in `criticalEffects.ts:49` referred to the hit-location penalty halving, which was fixed by C4. They target the nearest enemy via `findTarget()`. CE ref: `party.cc partyMemberGetCombatants`. Outstanding: formation pathfinding. **Companion level-up FIXED 2026-10-06** (`Party.incLevels`, port of `party_member.cc:1454 _partyMemberIncLevels` + `_partyMemberCopyLevelInfo`, driven by `party.txt` level_minimum/level_up_every/level_pids, called per PC level-up like `stat.cc:789`; misc.msg 9000 + per-member float lines; persisted in saves). **CHA cap removed 2026-10-06**: CE `partyMemberAdd` has no follower limit — scripts enforce it (party.h macros, incl. Magnetic Personality); the DH2 engine cap could veto a scripted recruit. Also: player level-up now applies Swift Learner (+5%/rank XP), Lifegiver per rank, and stat.msg 600; `float_msg` now floats over the object passed in (was always the script's self_obj). **Formation FIXED 2026-10-07**: `placeParty` is now CE `_partyMemberSyncPosition` (visible critter members on alternating sides at distance 1, 1, 2, 2, …; `_objPMAttemptPlacement`'s spiral with blocked/reachable checks). Following is script-driven via `critter_p_proc` (63c0c8f), and combat uses AI packets, as in CE. | `src/combat/Combat.ts` | `combat.cc`, `party.cc` | major | fixed |

| C17 | **Line of fire, friendly fire and blast radius were missing. FIXED 2026-10-07** — bursts spread over a fixed 3-line cone to a few hexes past the target; a missed shot rolled -70 against critters up to 5 hexes past the target; explosive and grenade hits damaged only the target; the AI picked burst when "≥2 enemies in range". Ported from CE: `_compute_spray` (rounds split centre/left/right, main-target rolls, crit +20), `_shoot_along_path` (per-critter to-hit along each line, so team-mates in the way get hit, max 6 extras), the `attackCompute` ranged tail (the shot flies on to `_tile_num_beyond` and the first shoot-blocker past the target takes 1 round ×2; grenades scatter up to distance/2), `_check_ranged_miss` (ported with its vanilla never-hits quirk), and `_compute_explosion_on_extras` (ring walk, grenade radius 2 / rocket 3, attacker backwash, shot-blocked check). On the AI side: `_ai_pick_hit_mode` (area_attack_mode and secondary_freq; `always` was being parsed as no_pref) and `_combat_safety_invalidate_weapon` (simulated spray or blast radius vs team-mates, CE's literal DR×(max−DT) test). New `src/combat/lineOfFire.ts` (`combatIsShotBlocked`) and `GameMap.straightPathObstacle` (full `_make_straight_path_func` with a start tile and a previous obstacle). Outstanding: CE `_ai_move_away` to a safe distance and `_ai_switch_weapons` (DH2 falls back from burst to single, else holds fire); explosions from DAM_EXPLODE critical failures. | `src/combat/Combat.ts`, `src/combat/lineOfFire.ts`, `src/map/GameMap.ts`, `src/aiPackets.ts` | `combat.cc attackCompute, _compute_spray, _shoot_along_path, _check_ranged_miss, _compute_explosion_on_extras, _combat_is_shot_blocked, _combat_safety_invalidate_weapon_func`; `combat_ai.cc _ai_pick_hit_mode` | major | fixed |
| C18 | **Hit chance drifted from CE `attackDetermineToHit`. FIXED 2026-10-07** — the partial-cover penalty was a hex-line count that also applied to melee and unarmed; the distance modifier applied to melee, never gave CE's close-range bonus, and ignored Long Range/Scope Range (and handled Sharpshooter wrongly); a crippled-arm -40 that CE doesn't have was applied. Missing entirely: min-ST -20 per point (Weapon Handling -3), Accurate +20, One Hander ±, the AC floor of 0, multihex target +15, knocked-down/out target +40, and combat difficulty ±20 for non-team attackers. `getHitChance` is now a straight port; attack type comes from the proto attack mode (`weaponAttackType`, CE `_attack_subtype`). | `src/combat/hitChance.ts` | `combat.cc:4313 attackDetermineToHit` | major | fixed |
| C19 | **who-hit-me / retaliation and the reaction hit were missing. FIXED 2026-10-07** — DH2 set `whoHitMe` only from scripts (`attack_setup`). CE's damage application records who hit a dead or knocked-out victim, and lets the intended target (or an enemy hit by accident) retaliate against a higher-rated attacker (`_combatai_rating`: max(melee, weapon max damage) + AC). `_critter_set_who_hit_me` sets the victim's reaction (LVAR 0) to −3 when the player hits it; this is CE's only engine-side reaction change (`_reaction_influence_` returns 0). Ported in `src/combat/whoHitMe.ts` and called from every damage path. | `src/combat/whoHitMe.ts`, `src/combat/Combat.ts` | `critter.cc:1285 _critter_set_who_hit_me`; `combat_ai.cc _combatai_check_retaliation, _combatai_rating`; `combat.cc:4706` | major | fixed |
| C20 | **Death animations and sounds were not CE. FIXED 2026-10-12** — DH2 picked the death by damage type alone with wrong art codes (plain death = blood-pool fall `bo` played directly, laser → `bg` dancing autofire, burst → `bj` burned to nothing, plasma always melted, electrical always electrified-to-nothing), ignored damage thresholds, Bloody Mess, attacker animation and fallbacks, forced an explosion on every critical death (which didn't even kill: DAM_DEAD criticals left the target alive), spawned an invented blood-pool object, played a random `hmxxxxba/bb/bd` on every death, and gave script kills (kill_critter, poison, radiation) a full animation. New `src/critter/deathAnim.ts` ports `_pick_death` (thresholds 15/45, molotov 5/15, Pyromaniac, Flameboy, Bloody Mess, violence level, `gNormal/gMaximumBloodDeathAnimations`), `_check_death`, `_pick_fall`, `_action_blood` (fall → blood-pool fall), the fire dance → burned-to-nothing chain, script damage's max-blood anim, and `critterKill`'s single-frame corpses (`_obj_fix_violence_settings`, `kill_critter_type` ftList + `_correctDeath`). The DIE sound is built from the picked anim (`actions.cc:321`). Also fixed: knockdown/get-up art codes (`ap`/`aq`/`ar`/`as` → `ba`/`bb`/`cj`/`ch`), the invented 30% dodge on a hit, and `StatSet.getBase` reading a stored 0 as the default (a critter at exactly 0 HP had 1 HP and lived). ## 3. Map System & Script Events

<!-- audited: 2026-09-30 — M6 added: objects added to a map outside the normal load loop (mapLoader.ts car/trunk injection, scripting.ts create_object_sid, criticalEffects/effects.ts droppedWeapon) rendered on top of everything and looked walkable-through, because a raw position assignment before push() skips the z-order splice (Obj.move()/objectZOrder or zsort()) that map-loaded objects get for free from JSON scan order. All three sites fixed; blood-pool decals (critter/lifecycle.ts) checked and confirmed harmless (OBJECT_FLAT draws in an always-first pass). -->

| ID | Description | File(s) | CE Reference | Sev | Status |
|----|-------------|---------|--------------|-----|--------|
| M0 | **Missing `.images.json` threw fatally, corrupting all subsequent map loads.** `loadNewMap` propagated a 404 exception synchronously, leaving `this.objects = null` and `isLoading = true` permanently. Any following map load then crashed on `serialize()` calling `.map()` on null. | `src/map/mapLoader.ts` | — | major | **FIXED 2026-06-02** |
| M0b | **`serialize()` called when `objects === null` after a failed previous load.** Guard at `loadMap` dirty-cache branch now checks `this.objects !== null` before serializing. | `src/map/mapLoader.ts` | — | major | **FIXED 2026-06-02** |
| M1 | **Spatial triggers persist across save/load.** FIXED (Phase 1/3/7 sprint, 2026-06-02) — `SerializedMap.spatials` now stores all elevations; `deserialize()` re-creates spatials from script names and reapplies saved LVARs. CE ref: `map.cc spatialLoad()`. | `src/map/GameMap.ts` (`deserialize`) | `map.cc spatialLoad()` | major | fixed |
| M2 | **`map_enter_p_proc` on elevation change.** VERIFIED CORRECT 2026-06-02 — CE `mapSetElevation` (map.cc:362) calls only `scriptsExecMapUpdateProc()`, never `map_enter_p_proc`. DH2's `changeElevation` calls `updateMap()` which fires `map_update_p_proc`. Behaviour is correct; TODO comment in `src/map/GameMap.ts` can be ignored. | `src/map/GameMap.ts` | `map.cc:362 mapSetElevation()` | minor | fixed |
| M3 | **Scripting engine not notified when `objectsAndSpatials` updates.** FIXED 2026-06-02 — `addObject()` now calls `obj.enterMap()` after inserting, firing `map_enter_p_proc` for newly-spawned scripted objects. `enterMap()` is a no-op for objects without scripts. | `src/map/GameMap.ts` | — | minor | fixed |
| M4 | **`map_exit_p_proc` now declared and fired.** FIXED 2026-06-02 — added `map_exit_p_proc` to `Script` class proc prototype list; `loadNewMap()` calls it on the outgoing map script before teardown, matching CE `map.cc:1440 scriptsExecMapExitProc()`. | `src/scripting.ts:420`, `src/map/mapLoader.ts` | `scripts.cc:2673 scriptsExecMapUpdateScripts()`; `scripts.h:65 SCRIPT_PROC_MAP_EXIT` | minor | fixed |
| M5 | **Missing/corrupt main map JSON also threw fatally (M0's sibling bug, same class but a different call site).** FIXED 2026-06-18 — M0 (2026-06-02) only guarded the `.images.json` fetch; the main `maps/<name>.json` fetch a few lines later in `loadNewMap` had no try/catch, so a missing or 0-byte map file (e.g. `maps/mbase34.json` — "Military Base Levels 3-4" — currently ships as an empty 0-byte file, a failed/incomplete asset extraction, **not yet re-extracted**) threw `SyntaxError: Unexpected end of JSON input` uncaught, leaving `isLoading=true` permanently and freezing input (`input.ts:49/176`) and the autocrawler (which polls `isLoading` with a timeout instead of failing fast). Now wrapped in try/catch: on failure, resets `isLoading=false` and shows a `showAlert()` dialog naming the map instead of crashing. The underlying empty `mbase34.json` itself is an asset-pipeline gap, not an engine bug — needs re-running extraction for that map; do not fabricate map data. | `src/map/mapLoader.ts` | — | major | fixed — asset gap closed by M7 (mbase34 re-exported) |
| M6 | **Objects added to a map at runtime (outside the normal map-load loop) render on top of literally everything and can visually appear walkable-through.** FIXED 2026-09-30 — found via user report that the injected Highwayman car (see W8) rendered above foreground scenery and the player, and looked walkable-through. Root cause: DH2's renderer is a plain painter's-algorithm draw in array order — `Renderer.renderObjects()` (`src/renderer.ts`) has no depth buffer and no per-tile sort, so draw order *is* array order. Objects loaded from a map's own JSON arrive already in the correct order; anything added later must be explicitly re-spliced into correct z-order via `Obj.move()` (which calls the insertion-sort in `objectZOrder()`, `src/object/Obj.ts`) or `zsort()` (full re-sort, same file) — a raw `obj.position = ...` assignment followed by `GameMap.addObject()`/`.push()` skips that step entirely and leaves the object stuck at the tail of the array, drawn last, on top of everything, every frame, regardless of its actual tile. Collision was **not** actually broken — `GameMap.recalcPath()` rescans the live object array fresh on every call with no cache to go stale, so a pushed object blocks correctly as soon as it exists; the "walks through it" perception was the same rendering bug wearing a different hat (the player sprite drawing on top of/through the misplaced object made it look passable). Fixed at three call sites doing the same raw-assign-then-push pattern: `src/map/mapLoader.ts` (car + trunk injection, both the clean-load and dirty-cache-revisit paths, plus `repositionExistingTrunk()`) — push then `.move()`; `src/scripting.ts` `create_object_sid()` (the general-purpose script intrinsic used by map scripts to spawn objects at runtime) — push then `zsort()` on the *target* elevation's array specifically, since `elev` isn't always the currently-displayed elevation and `.move()` only re-splices the current one; `src/criticalEffects/effects.ts` `droppedWeapon()` (weapon-drop-on-critical-disarm) — push then `.move()`. Verified live for all three: object index checked directly (never at array tail after the fix), a cross-elevation `create_object_sid()` call confirmed it doesn't corrupt the wrong elevation's array, and screenshots confirmed correct bidirectional occlusion (player rendering both behind and in front of the car depending on relative position). **Not fixed, confirmed harmless**: `src/critter/lifecycle.ts` blood-pool decals have the same raw-assign-then-push pattern, but decals set `OBJECT_FLAT` (`flags & 0x8`), which the renderer draws in an always-first pass regardless of array position — no visible symptom, left as-is. | `src/map/mapLoader.ts`, `src/scripting.ts`, `src/criticalEffects/effects.ts` | `tile.cc:854 tileIsInFrontOf` (the CE depth-order reference `hexIsInFrontOf`/`objectZCompare` already implement) | major | fixed |
| M7 | **Five map JSONs shipped as 0-byte files. FIXED 2026-10-06** — root cause was `tools/fomap.py getCritterArtPath()` raising `"reindex"` for critter FIDs with aliased/death animations (electrify, burned-to-nothing, fire dance, called-shot pic); ported CE `art.cc:615 artBuildFilePath` + `_art_get_code` + `artAliasFid` (also in runtime `src/pro.ts`, which had the same throw). `exportMap` now parses before opening the output so a failure can't leave a 0-byte file. mbase34, klacanyn and rndparih (Special Pariahs Encounter, `maps.txt` Map 107) re-exported and load; arcaves/denbus1/newr1/ncr1/navarro re-export byte-identical. `newr1a`/`newr2a` are unreferenced, truncated dev maps in master.dat (parse past EOF / bad script index) — JSON stubs deleted. Original note: `maps/mbase34.json` and `maps/klacanyn.json` are listed in `data/data/maps.txt` and so are reachable in play; `maps/newr1a.json`, `maps/newr2a.json`, `maps/rndparih.json` are not referenced by `maps.txt` (likely unused DAT leftovers). | `maps/*.json`, `tools/fomap.py`, `src/pro.ts` | `art.cc:615 artBuildFilePath`, `art.cc:904 artAliasFid` | major | fixed |
| M8 | **MVARs were wiped on every map load. FIXED 2026-10-07** — `Scripting.reset()` set `mapVars = {}` on each load (and straight after a save restored them). Every town's map-var state reset on every revisit. MVARs now persist per map; defaults only fill vars that are missing. | `src/scripting.ts` | `map.cc` (MVARs saved with the map) | critical | fixed |
| M9 | **`map_exit_p_proc` only ran for the map script, after the snapshot. FIXED 2026-10-07** — now runs on every script on the map (party excluded), drains the deferred removal queue, then snapshots; it also runs on dirty-cache transitions, which previously skipped it. CE ref: `map.cc:1427 _map_save_in_game` → `scripts.cc scriptsExecMapUpdateScripts(MAP_EXIT)`. | `src/map/mapLoader.ts` | `map.cc:1427` | major | fixed |
| M10 | **Party members were saved into the map snapshot. FIXED 2026-10-07** — `GameMap.serialize` excluded only the player, so revisiting a map (or loading a save) restored a second copy of each companion and the car trunk. The whole party is now excluded. A same-elevation re-entry also no longer re-runs `placeParty()` over scripts' `move_to`. | `src/map/GameMap.ts` | `map.cc _partyMemberPrepLoad` before map save | major | fixed |
| M11 | **Map variables missing on 10 maps, wrong on 3. FIXED 2026-10-15.**<br>**Missing:** `tools/parse_gam.py` only accepted a `//(N)` index comment. Arroyo Village (`// 0`) and nine other maps got no `.mvars.json` at all.<br>**Wrong:** `depolva` lost one var, `enctrp` lost a value written without `;`, and `newr2` stored its single var at index 1.<br>**Fix:** the parser now follows CE `globalVarsRead`: after the section header, each line that is not empty and not `//` is the next variable, in file order. It writes `{}` for maps without vars, so the engine sees no 404s, and `loadMapVars` reads each file once per session.<br>**Globals:** `data/gvars.json` now comes from `data/data/vault13.gam`, the file CE loads (`data/maps/vault13.gam` is the Vault 13 map). It has 695 vars instead of 694: the file's comments skip "(129)", so the old parser left index 129 undefined.<br>**Pipeline:** both steps now run in `tools/setup.py` as the `convert_gam` stage, together with a new `write_config` stage that creates `fallout2.cfg`. | `tools/parse_gam.py`; `src/scripting.ts` | `game.cc globalVarsRead`; `map.cc mapLoad` (MAP_GLOBAL_VARS:) | major | **FIXED 2026-10-15** |
| M12 | **Two map tracebacks in every pipeline run. FIXED 2026-10-23** — the vanilla data ships `newr1a.map` and `newr2a.map`, leftovers in an older, unreadable layout (script counts like 50332158, tile indexes past `tiles.lst`). Neither is in `data/data/maps.txt`, and CE loads maps only through it (`map.cc` `mapLoadById` / `_map_get_index_of`), so the game never uses them. `export_maps` now holds back fomap's output and, for an unlisted map that fails, prints one "Skipped map … not in data/data/maps.txt" line instead of the debug lines, two tracebacks and a WARNING. Listed maps still fail loudly. | `tools/setup.py` | `map.cc mapLoadById` | minor | fixed |

Metarule ops 30 (`wmCarCurrentArea` → returns `_currentCarAreaId`) / 31 (`GIVE_CAR_TO_PARTY` → `setIsInCar(true)` + `fillCarFuel()` + `GVAR_PLAYER_GOT_CAR=1` + `uiWorldMap()` — CE ref: `wmCarGiveToParty → mapSetTransition(map=-2) → wmWorldMap()`) / 32 (`GIVE_CAR_GAS`) and metarule3 op 110 (`wmCarIsOutOfGas`) wired. `currentCarAreaId` tracked on town entry: `ui_worldmap.ts` sets `isInCar=false` and records parked area. Worldmap UI shows car image (`#wmCarImage` / `art/intrface/wmcarmve.png`) and fuel bar (`#wmCarFuelBar`) when in car; globe overlay (`#wmGlobeOverlay`) when not. Car state (incl. `currentCarAreaId`) persists via save/load. Console command `window.giveCar([fuel])` for testing. **Car visual on local maps FIXED 2026-09-16, extended with trunk object + data-driven parking (later commit, undated in this registry)** — F2 map scripts (`map_enter_p_proc`) place the Highwayman dynamically; DH2 replicates this in `src/map/mapLoader.ts` (`loadNewMap()` clean-load path AND `loadMap()` dirty-cache-revisit path — both re-inject the transient car body; only the clean-load path also injects the trunk, since the trunk is not `_transient` and survives dirty-cache serialization). When `GVAR_PLAYER_GOT_CAR` is set and the car is parked at the area containing the loaded map, a `Scenery.fromPID(0x020003F1)` (car body, PROTO_ID_CAR) and an `Obj.fromPID(455)` (trunk container, PROTO_ID_CAR_TRUNK) are injected. Car position resolves via `lut/car_parking.json` (per-map `{carTile, trunkTile}` override table, keyed by map name) when the entry is ≥ 0, else an entrance-offset heuristic (`entrance.x+3`, trunk at `car.x+2`). The car body's `_script.use_p_proc` sets `isInCar=true` and emits `Events.emit('openWorldmap')` (decoupled via `events.ts` to avoid circular import; handler registered in `main.ts`). CE ref: `worldmap.cc:6043 wmCarGiveToParty`, `proto_instance.cc:465 car examine`, `proto_instance.cc:1218 trunk also refuelable`. **Car body tile-collision aspect RESOLVED 2026-09-23 for 10 towns** — `lut/car_parking.json`'s `carTile` values for `denbus1`, `broken1`, `kladwtwn`, `modmain`, `gecksetl`, `newr1`, `reddown`, `navarro`, `sfchina`, `ncrent`, `vctyctyd` are no longer placeholders: they were extracted directly from the original compiled `map_enter_p_proc` bytecode (`data/scripts/<map>.int`) via a static disassembly pass (methodology + per-map citations in `car_system.md` §7) — these are the original developers' own values, not heuristic guesses, so no in-browser verification is needed for them. Two towns (`ncrent`, `vctyctyd`) were missing from the candidate list entirely and have been added; `klamall`/`ncr1`/`ncr2` were wrong map-name guesses (real submaps are `kladwtwn`/`ncrent`) and are now documented as such rather than silently wrong. `denbus2`, `broken2`, `newr2` were confirmed to genuinely have no car-placement script. **Trunk placement bugs FIXED 2026-09-29** — three issues found via user testing after the above: (1) the dirty-cache revisit path only ever re-injected the car body, never the trunk — a map visited once *before* the car was ever parked there (e.g. `giveCar()` called while already standing on it) got cached without a trunk and stayed that way forever on every later revisit; fixed by checking for a missing trunk in `loadMap()`'s dirty-cache branch and injecting one on demand, same as the clean-load path. (2) The `car.x+2` tile-arithmetic heuristic didn't land on a real adjacent hex — DH2's tile grid is an offset/staggered hex grid (`src/geometry/hexGrid.ts` `hexNeighbors`), so `+2 x, +0 y` isn't reachable by any actual hex walk and rendered visibly skewed. Replaced with a screen-space offset (`tileToScreen`/`tileFromScreen` round-trip), tuned interactively in-game to `(0, -80)` screen px (trunk directly above the car). (3) Added `window.setTrunkOffset(x, y)` (main.ts) for live-tuning without a rebuild — repositions an already-injected trunk on the current map immediately via `repositionExistingTrunk()`, no leave/re-enter needed. Metarule 52/53 (`SET`/`GET_CAR_CARRY_AMOUNT`) are still hardcoded no-ops rather than reading/writing the trunk proto's `maxSize`, unlike CE. See `car_system.md` §4 for the full remaining gap list (trunk built via generic `Obj.fromPID` instead of `Item.fromPID`; no car-upgrade GVAR speed tiers; no cross-elevation parking awareness; the 5 remaining towns' `carTile` still unverified). **Car rendering above everything + walkable-through FIXED 2026-09-30 — see M6** (§3): the injected car/trunk objects were never re-spliced into z-order after being pushed into the map's object array, so they always drew on top of literally everything; collision was already correct, the "walk through it" look was the same rendering bug. | `src/worldmap/{Worldmap,types,encounters}.ts`, `src/worldmap.ts`, `src/ui_worldmap.ts`, `src/saveload.ts`, `src/scripting.ts`, `src/map/mapLoader.ts`, `lut/car_parking.json`, `src/main.ts`, `play.html`, `ui.css` | `worldmap.cc:3028,5984,6037,6043,6179`; `proto_instance.cc:465,1218`; `proto_types.h:184,195`; `interpreter_extra.cc:3234-3241,3331-3347`; `data/scripts/*.int` (see car_system.md §7) | major | fixed — superseded by W14 (2026-10-07): placement is script-driven, metarule 52/53 done |
| W9 | **Area entrance hotspots offset by (22, 21) px. FIXED 2026-07-27** — CE `worldmap.cc wmTownMapInit()` calls `buttonCreate(wmBkWin, entrance->x, entrance->y, ...)` where coordinates are in the 640×480 window frame, but the town FRM is blitted at `(WM_VIEW_X=22, WM_VIEW_Y=21)`. DH2 placed the hotspot div at `(entrance.x, entrance.y)` within `#areamap` whose background image starts at (0,0) — 22px too far right, 21px too far down. Fixed by positioning at `(entrance.x - 22, entrance.y - 21)`. CE ref: `worldmap.cc:5886 wmTownMapInit()`, `WM_VIEW_X=22, WM_VIEW_Y=21`. | `src/ui_worldmap.ts` | `worldmap.cc:5886 wmTownMapInit()` | minor | fixed |
| W10 | **Walk masks not loaded.** FIXED 2026-07-04 — `parser.ts` captures `walk_mask_name` per `[Tile N]` section into `Worldmap.walkMaskNames[0..19]`; `Worldmap.ts` adds `worldPosInvalid(x, y)` (direct port of CE's `wmWorldPosInvalid`, `worldmap.cc:4244`), lazily fetching `.msk` files via `getFileBinarySync` and caching per name. `updateWorldmapPlayer()` halts travel when blocked. **Follow-up 2026-07-05**: asset path corrected from `data/{name}.msk` → `data/data/{name}.msk` (CE stores these under `data\` in the DAT archive, so the extracted path is `data/data/`). | `src/worldmap/{Worldmap,parser,types}.ts` | `worldmap.cc:1337,4208,4244,4335-4341`; `worldmap.txt walk_mask_name` | minor | fixed |

Labels use `position: relative; height: 27px` for natural stacking; scroll via `outer.scrollTop`; `wmtabs.png` background kept in sync by updating `backgroundPositionY = (-27 - scrollY)`. CE ref: `worldmap.cc wmMakeTabsLabelList`. | `src/ui_worldmap.ts`, `ui.css` | `worldmap.cc wmMakeTabsLabelList()` | minor | fixed |
| W12 | **Worldmap does not scroll / pan when stationary.** FIXED 2026-06-15 — `#worldmap` sized to 1400×1500 px and panned via `transform: translate(-panX, -panY)` clipped by `#worldMapWorld overflow:hidden`. Arrow/WASD keys and mouse-edge (20 px threshold) pan at 8 px/tick when not traveling. View centres on player at map open and auto-tracks during travel. Click handler fixed: `getBoundingClientRect()` already returns visual (transformed) position so `_panX`/`_panY` must NOT be re-added. CE ref: `worldmap.cc wmInterfaceScrollTabArea`. | `src/worldmap/Worldmap.ts`, `ui.css` | `worldmap.cc wmInterfaceScrollTabArea()` | major | fixed |
| W13 | **City-entry hotspot marker (green triangle) unreliable — offset from the city circle, and its hitbox included dead transparent corners.** FIXED 2026-09-24 — Two independent bugs in `#worldmapTarget` positioning/hit-testing, found via user report ("triangle click just walks me there instead of entering") and confirmed live: (1) `centerWorldmapTarget()` used `$worldmapTarget.offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` to center the marker on the arrival position, but the very first call happens from `init()` while the worldmap panel is still `display:none` — under a hidden ancestor `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` both read `0`, silently degrading "centered" into the old uncentered top-left placement (marker rendered ~12×6px off from the city circle) for the first marker shown each session. Fixed by using the fixed CSS-declared size (25×13, `WORLDMAP_TARGET_W`/`H` constants) instead of live layout measurement. (2) The 25×13 hit-test rect included the image's fully-transparent corners outside the actual triangle silhouette (traced from the source PNG pixels: full-width top edge tapering linearly to a point at bottom-center). Added `clip-path: polygon(0% 0%, 100% 0%, 50% 100%)` via a `.wmHotspotShape` class, toggled on only while `hotspot1.png`/`hotspot2.png` (the triangle) is shown — not for `wmaptarg.png` (the crosshair shown while still walking to the target, a different shape). Verified live: marker now centers exactly on the city circle; `elementFromPoint()` confirmed the clipped corners correctly fall through to the map underneath while the triangle body/apex remain clickable; full click → `uiWorldMapShowArea()` flow re-verified end-to-end. | `src/worldmap/Worldmap.ts`, `ui.css` | `worldmap.cc:3124-3157` (hotspot press/release behavior) | major | fixed |

FIXED 2026-10-07** — DH2 injected the car and trunk from `lut/car_parking.json`, but the town scripts (`create_object_sid(PID_CAR…)`) and the trunk script (`party_add`, `move_to`) already do it, as in CE. Result: two cars and two trunks. The injection, `carParking.ts`, the LUT and `carMapName` were removed. Also ported: speed tiers (blower/New Reno upgrade/super car), `wmCarUseGas` reductions, out-of-gas handling (`CITY_CAR_OUT_OF_GAS`), and `wmCarGiveToParty` (no refill, msg 1502). See `wiki/car_system.md` §0. | `src/worldmap/Worldmap.ts`, `src/map/mapLoader.ts`, `src/scripting.ts` | `worldmap.cc:3025-3082, wmCarUseGas, wmCarGiveToParty` | major | fixed |

FIXED 2026-10-07** — it only edited `globalState.knownAreas`; the worldmap draws areas whose `state` is set and built its markers once, at init. Now a port of `opMarkAreaKnown`: type 0 shows the town (CITY_STATE_INVISIBLE −66 hides it) and sets the visited state; type 1 marks the area of a map visited. Random encounters in the car now set the car area the same way (`:3111`). | P11 | **Camera re-centers on the dialogue target every time `uiStartDialogue()` fires, including on Barter/Trade/Control return — causing a visible ~1-hex camera jump.** FIXED 2026-06-22. CE centers the camera on the dialogue speaker once, at `gameDialogEnter`, and the entire simulation is frozen for the duration of the modal dialogue loop, so the target never moves. DH2 does not pause world ticking while dialogue/barter/companion-control UI is open, so a companion can drift a hex via `followPlayer()` while a sub-screen was open; `uiStartDialogue()`'s recenter-on-target logic (`ui_dialogue.ts`) ran unconditionally on every call, including the one triggered by `start_gdialog()` when `Scripting.reenterDialogue()` re-runs `talk_p_proc()` on return from a sub-screen, recentering on the now-shifted position and producing a visible jump. Fix: the recenter block now also skips when `#dialogueBox` was already showing (same `alreadyShowing` flag added for P10's double-animate fix), so it only runs on a genuinely fresh dialogue entry. Not implemented: actually pausing world simulation during dialogue, which is the deeper CE-accurate fix but a larger change — out of scope for this pass. | `src/ui_dialogue.ts` | `game_dialog.cc gameDialogEnter` (one-time centering; world frozen during dialogue, unlike DH2) | minor | fixed |

The button stayed invisible/non-interactive even when "shown." Fix: use an explicit non-none value, `'block'`, instead of `''`. | `src/ui_dialogue.ts` | n/a — DH2-only CSS/JS bug | major | fixed |

| P22 | **Dialogue window's talking-head display had no "screen curvature" glint/shadow overlay — user-recalled `hilight1.png`/`hilight2.png` effect.** FIXED 2026-06-23/24. CE's `gameDialogRenderTalkingHead()` (`game_dialog.cc:4549-4627`) renders a 388x200 head-display rect at (126,14) inside the dialogue background window (DH2: `#dialogueContainer`, 640x480) — either the speaker's head animation, or a live crop of the isometric game view if no head art exists (`:4605-4627`, **separate gap, still not implemented**). Every frame, `gameDialogRenderHighlight()` (`:4526-4546`) blends two fixed-position glints: upper at (426,15) using `hilight1.frm` (89×81px, lightening) and lower at (129,81) using `hilight2.frm` (137×131px, darkening with a warm amber/vacuum-tube tint — user confirmed the CE game showed a yellowish-orange colour there, consistent with warm cathode tube glow leaking behind the glass). **Asset pipeline**: wrote standalone `tools/export_mask_frms.py` (does NOT touch `tools/frmpixels.py`/`tools/exportImagesPar.py`) that re-exports these three FRMs from `data/art/intrface/` without palette lookup. Final encoding after several corrective iterations (each confirmed by pixel inspection): (1) `egg.png` — white RGBA, alpha = raw byte rescaled from 0-128 to 0-255, **then inverted for non-zero pixels** (`255 - scaled`) so the oval CENTER (raw bytes ~1, CE spatial inspection confirmed low-center/high-perimeter gradient) gets high alpha → transparent wall, and the perimeter gets low alpha → opaque wall, matching the "center reveals player" behaviour; (2) `hilight1.png` — R=G=B=255, A=`(255-raw)*1.0` (inverted per CE's `(256-v)>>4` direction where low raw = strongest effect), plain semi-transparent white — lighten overlay by alpha compositing; (3) `hilight2.png` — R=255,G=140,B=30 (warm amber), A=`(255-raw)*1.0` — plain semi-transparent amber glow, matches user memory of yellowish-orange. `HIGHLIGHT_STRENGTH` and `HILIGHT2_COLOR` are named constants in the script for re-tuning. **Rendering**: `#dialogueHighlightUpper`/`#dialogueHighlightLower` permanent `<img>` children of `#dialogueContainer` (`play.html`, `ui.css`, `opacity:1`) — plain alpha compositing (no `mix-blend-mode`, which proved unreliable: `screen(white)=white` regardless of backdrop; `multiply(white)=identity`). CSS `opacity` is the sole runtime strength knob — full 0-1 range, tunable via `setDialogueHighlights(upper, lower)` in console (`src/main.ts`). **Egg texture cache**: version-bumped URL in `src/render/webglContext.ts` (`?v=20260623b`) to force browser to discard any cached WebGL texture on reload. Also fixed the pre-existing binary-cutoff egg: old hand-patched egg.png was alpha=0/1 (no gradient); now a real 0-243 falloff. **Known simplification**: CE's exact per-palette-index gray-table blend (`_light_BlendTable`/`_dark_BlendTable`) is not ported — plain alpha compositing of a white glint and amber shadow approximates the visual effect. | P10 | **Two follow-on bugs surfaced testing P6-P8's fix: `dialogueReply()` only special-cased Barter, not Combat Control; and re-entering dialogue double-animated.** FIXED 2026-06-22. (1) `dialogueReply()` (`scripting.ts:269-284`) only skipped its "no options left → close dialogue" auto-exit when `uiMode === UIMode.barter`. Picking `[Combat Control]` sets `uiMode = UIMode.companionControl` instead, so the check fell through to `dialogueExit()` immediately — hiding `#dialogueContainer`/`#dialogueBox` instantly with no animation (the reported "TV screen vanishes abruptly") and, worse, nulling `currentDialogueObject` and resuming the VM while Control was still opening. That null is why clicking Talk afterward to return found `reenterDialogue()` silently no-op'ing (its `if (!currentDialogueObject...) return` guard) — dialogue reappeared with the correct slide animation but stayed frozen, since nothing ever ran `talk_p_proc` again. Fix: condition broadened to `if (globalState.uiMode !== UIMode.dialogue) return`, covering Combat Control/Trade/any future non-dialogue screen, not just Barter specifically. (2) Returning from Barter/Trade/Control to dialogue manually slides `#dialogueBox` up *and* calls `Scripting.reenterDialogue()`, which re-runs `talk_p_proc` — and `talk_p_proc` typically starts with `start_gdialog()` (`scripting.ts:1727`), which calls `uiStartDialogue()` **unconditionally**, resetting `#dialogueBox` to its origin and re-animating it a second time (the reported "moves up, vanishes, moves up again"). Fix: `uiStartDialogue()` (`ui_dialogue.ts:58`) now checks whether `#dialogueBox` is already `visibility:visible` and skips the redundant reset+re-animate if so — idempotent regardless of which opcode (`start_gdialog` unconditional vs. `gsay_start` mode-guarded) triggers it. | `scripting.ts:269-284`, `ui_dialogue.ts:58-70` | `game_dialog.cc` dialogue loop (DH2-only state bugs, no direct CE equivalent) | major | fixed |
| P5 | **Party member control & customization screens added; trigger mechanism corrected 2026-06-21.** ADDED 2026-06-18 — `uiCompanionControl()`/`uiCompanionCustomize()` implement CE's `partyMemberControlWindowInit`/`partyMemberCustomizationWindowInit` (`game_dialog.cc`): 5 disposition presets (Berserk/Aggressive/Defensive/Coward/Custom) via `setCompanionDisposition()`, 6-category Custom screen (area-attack/run-away/best-weapon/distance/attack-who/chem-use) via `setCompanionCustomSetting()`, weight-based trade (no money, matching `inventory.cc:4710-4722`) via `uiCompanionTrade()`. All button positions/FRM IDs verified directly against `game_dialog.cc` source, not inferred — see `wiki/companion_party.md` §8 for the full citation table. Surfaced and fixed a real pre-existing bug in the process: `aiPackets.ts`'s `Disposition` type/parser was missing `'defensive'` entirely (see `wiki/ai_behavior.md`). **2026-06-21 correction**: the original 2026-06-18 wiring was based on an unverified assumption ("DH2 has no companion dialogue scripts") and made Talk bypass dialogue entirely to jump straight to the control screen. In-game testing (talking to Sulik after recruiting him) showed this was wrong — Sulik *does* have a `talk_p_proc` (`data/scripts/kcsulik.int`). Direct read of `raw/fallout2-ce/src/game_dialog.cc` confirms CE never skips dialogue for party members: `gameDialogEnter()` (line 732) just sets `gGameDialogSpeakerIsPartyMember`, and the only effect is that `_gdialog_window_create()` (lines 4357-4388) adds an extra **Combat Control** button (593,116, `gameDialogCombatControlButtonOnMouseUp`) to the normal dialogue window, alongside the existing Trade/Barter button — clicking it (not Talk itself) is what opens `partyMemberControlWindowInit`. Fix: reverted the bypass in `input.ts` and `ui_contextmenu.ts` (Talk now always calls `Scripting.talk()` normally, same as any NPC); added a synthesized `[Combat Control]` dialogue option in `scripting.ts`'s `gsay_end()` whenever `globalState.gParty.isPartyMember(currentDialogueObject)`, mirroring the existing `[Barter]` option pattern (`game_dialog.cc:3662 _gdCanBarter`) since DH2 has no literal dialogue-window button chrome to attach a real button to. **Known simplifications**: "Use Best Weapon"/"Use Best Armor" buttons are present (correct CE layout) but not implemented — CE's weapon/armor-quality heuristic (`_ai_search_inven_weap`/`_ai_search_inven_armor`) is its own substantial sub-system; the control screen's own Talk button closes the screen rather than re-entering the dialogue tree. | `src/ui_companion.ts`, `src/ui_companion_trade.ts`, `src/party.ts`, `src/aiPackets.ts`, `src/object/Critter.ts`, `src/combat/AI.ts`, `src/ui_panels.ts`, `src/input.ts`, `src/ui_contextmenu.ts`, `src/scripting.ts` | `game_dialog.cc:3354,~3780,732,2825-2830,3637-3644,4357-4388`; `combat_ai.cc:903 aiSetDisposition`; `inventory.cc:5031,4710-4722` | major | fixed |

## 8. Audio System

| ID | Description | File(s) | CE Reference | Sev | Status |
|----|-------------|---------|--------------|-----|--------|
| AU2 | **Speech and SFX played at double speed. FIXED 2026-10-11** — CE plays speech and SFX as mono; only the background music gets `soundSetChannels(…, 3)` (stereo) (`game_sound.cc:643`). Speech ACM headers claim 2 channels (`ssa2.acm`: 706990 samples, but its `.lip` counts 1413980 bytes = 32 s mono). `acm2wav` labels everything stereo, so SFX WAVs and speech MP3s were half as long. New `tools/acmMono.py` (raw decode via acm2wav, which is complete, or ffmpeg as fallback, since ffmpeg drops the tail of some files) writes them mono at the header rate, used by `convertAudio.py` (SFX) and `convertSpeech.py`. Music unchanged. All 1361 SFX and 1111 speech files reconverted; the lip-sync timing (bytes of 22 kHz mono) now matches. `AUDIO_ASSET_QUERY` busts stale browser caches. | `tools/acmMono.py`, `tools/convertAudio.py`, `tools/convertSpeech.py`, `src/audio.ts`, `src/talkingHead.ts` | `game_sound.cc:643`, `sound_decoder.cc` | major | fixed |
| AU3 | **Talking head drawn over the dialogue frame. FIXED 2026-10-11** — `alltlk.png` was the CSS background of `#dialogueContainer`, which always paints under its children, so the head canvas covered the frame. The frame is now its own `#dialogueFrame` layer above the head (the TV hole is transparent palette index 0), so its border clips the head as in CE; the highlights stay on top. | `play.html`, `ui.css`, `src/talkingHead.ts` | `game_dialog.cc` head window under the dialog frame | minor | fixed |
| AU4 | **Movie window could outlive the movie. FIXED 2026-10-11** — an `error` (e.g. a missing `.webm`) during the fade-in ran `finish()` before `start()` put the window up, which then stayed forever. The teardown also left the media pipeline alive (pause + remove only), and Chrome's own cast/picture-in-picture overlay on `<video>` could linger until a repaint (tab switch). Now every exit (end, skip, error) goes through one idempotent `finish()`: stop, `removeAttribute('src')` + `load()`, detach all listeners, remove the window (CE `movieStop`/`_movieClose` order). `start()` bails if already finished, and the video has controls, PiP and remote playback disabled. Verified: skip, natural end and a missing file during fade-in leave nothing in the DOM. The reported grey box couldn't be reproduced here; re-check in your browser. | `src/gameMovie.ts` | `movie.cc movieStop / _movieClose` | minor | fixed |
| AU5 | **Door/container sounds wrong, ladder silent. FIXED 2026-10-11** — `Obj.ts` used hard-coded names: open = `sndoorsa`, close = `sndoorsc` (both the *unlock* sound), locked = always variant A, a nonexistent `silcntna` for locked containers, and random container variants. New `src/sfxNames.ts` ports `game_sound.cc sfxBuildOpenName` (`S{O,C,L,N,U}DOORS<id>`, `I…CNTNR<id>`, <id> = proto sound byte; 0 = no file, as in CE), used for open/close/locked and by the `sfx_build_open_name` opcode. Climbing a ladder now plays `sfxBuildCharName(dude, ANIM_CLIMB_LADDER)` (`actions.cc:1050`) through CE's human fallback (`game_sound.cc:1115`: `HMWARRAE` → `HMXXXXAE`; female `H F XXXX…` then `HMXXXX…`). Verified: Den doors play `sodoorsc`/`scdoorsc`/`sldoorsc` by door id, and a Broken Hills ladder plays `hmxxxxae`. Not yet ported: the other `sfxBuildCharName` sites (put away/take out weapon, dodge, knockdown, death). | `src/sfxNames.ts`, `src/object/Obj.ts`, `src/scripting.ts`, `src/audio.ts` | `game_sound.cc sfxBuildOpenName / sfxBuildCharName / gameSoundPlayFile`, `proto_instance.cc:1712-1846`, `actions.cc:1050` | minor | fixed |
| AU6 | **Character action sounds missing. FIXED 2026-10-11** — only the ladder climb used `sfxBuildCharName`; unarmed hits played a weapon impact sound, misses `cmbtflx`, and dodge/hit/knockdown/death/weapon swap were silent. `sfxNames.ts` now implements the CE `extra` overrides (falls + PASS_OUT → `Y`, + DIE → `Z`; punch/kick + CONTACT → `Z`; TAKE_OUT takes the weapon code) and is wired at the CE call sites: punch/kick swing + contact (`actions.cc:625/637`), melee dodge (`:660`), hit from front/back (`:431`), knockdown fall (`:402`), death with DIE (`:321`), put away / take out (`interface.cc:1824`, `animation.cc:1188`). Human critters fall back to `H?XXXX…`. Not ported: put-away before a ladder climb (DH2 climbs without holstering), throw-anim sound for weaponless throws (`actions.cc:740`), knockback-death KNOCKDOWN variant. | `src/sfxNames.ts`, `src/combat/Combat.ts`, `src/critter/lifecycle.ts`, `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` | `game_sound.cc sfxBuildCharName`, `actions.cc _action_melee / _show_damage_to_object`, `interface.cc`, `animation.cc animationRegisterTakeOutWeapon` | minor | fixed |
| AU7 | **Death screen, ending slides, panning scene and quick-help screen in wrong colours. FIXED 2026-10-13** — the image export converted every FRM with `color.pal`, but 42 pictures in `art/intrface` ship their own palette (`<name>.pal` next to `<name>.frm`: `death`, `eg_*` ending slides, `dp` panning background, `seq*`, `helpscrn`, `arroyo01`), which CE loads before showing them (`endgame.cc:735 endgameEndingLoadPalette`, `main.cc:433`, `game.cc:1217`). `tools/exportImagesPar.py` now uses the sibling palette when there is one. Not a regression — the DarkFO exporter always did this. Re-run `export_images` (or the art is already re-exported locally). | `tools/exportImagesPar.py` | `endgame.cc endgameEndingLoadPalette`, `main.cc`, `game.cc`, `color.cc colorPaletteLoad` | minor | fixed |
| AU8 | **Sound effects lost their last ~0.1 s; acm2wav.exe needed. FIXED 2026-10-23** — with `acm2wav.exe` out of the repo, a fresh clone decoded ACM with ffmpeg's `interplay_acm`, which stops with decode errors ("Too large b", "get_buffer() failed") near the end of ~40 SFX and drops their last block (up to 0.09 s; 312 of 1361 files differed). CE's bit reader feeds zero bytes past EOF (`soundDecoderReadNextChunk`) and never fails there. New `tools/acmDecoder.py` ports `sound_decoder.cc` (`ReadBands`, `ReadBand_Fmt0/3–16/17–29/31`, `untransform_all` with the 16-bit level-0 wrap, `soundDecoderDecode`) and decodes SFX, music (interleaved stereo) and speech; ffmpeg is now only the MP3 encoder for speech. Verified: all 1361 SFX and 21 music tracks are sample-identical to the acm2wav output (~1.3 M samples/s). Also: `--delete-originals` listed every ACM twice (`*.acm` and `*.ACM` on a case-insensitive file system) and warned 2472 times; the list is deduped. | `tools/acmDecoder.py`, `tools/acmMono.py`, `tools/convertAudio.py`, `tools/convertSpeech.py`, `tools/setup.py` | `sound_decoder.cc` | minor | fixed |
| AU1 | **`rollNextSfx` crashed on null/undefined/empty `ambientSfx`.** Maps without ambient SFX entries return `ambientSfx: null` or `[]`; the old guard only checked `sfx.length` (throws on null), and `sfx[0][0]` would crash on empty arrays. Two guards added: early return on `!sfx` or `sfx.length === 0`, and early return when `sumFreqs === 0`. | `src/audio.ts` | `game_sound.cc` | major | **FIXED 2026-06-02** |

A `statsChanged` emit (`drugs.ts` — drug heal tick, trickle tick, or wear-off) firing while the player is mid native HTML5 drag of one of those exact `<img>` nodes destroys the node the browser's drag session is tracking out from under it, before `dragend`/`drop` ever dispatch. ## 15. FRM Animation Pipeline

| ID | Description | File(s) | CE Reference | Sev | Status |
|----|-------------|---------|--------------|-----|--------|
| FA0 | **`animInfo` missing `'single'` key — `singleAnimation()` critters froze.** `singleAnimation()` sets `this.anim = 'single'` for one-shot forward playback, but `'single'` was absent from `animInfo`, causing the tombstone guard to fire and freeze critters (e.g. nachldaa/nachldba on Klamath). Fixed: `'single': { type: 'static' }` added. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` | `animation.cc anim_run_sequence()` | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-02** |
| FA0b | **Unknown `anim` key caused `TypeError` on every rAF frame.** When `animInfo[this.anim]` was `undefined` (stale save state or script bug), accessing `.type` threw on every animation frame, hammering the console and locking the critter. Fixed: tombstone guard sets `anim='dead'`. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` | — | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-02** |
| FA0c | **`Obj` base class had no `hasAnimation()` method.** `scripting.ts:use_obj_on_obj` passes any `Obj` as source (via `as Critter` cast); non-Critter objects (encdet, encfite, encpres) crashed with `source.hasAnimation is not a function`. Fixed: `Obj.hasAnimation()` stub added returning `false`. | `src/object/Obj.ts` | — | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-02** |
| FA1 | **`updateStaticAnim` hardcodes fps = 8.** FIXED 2026-06-02 — changed to `globalState.imageInfo[this.art]?.fps \|\| 8`, matching `updateLoopingAnim`. CE ref: `art.cc:713 artGetFramesPerSecond()`. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` | `art.cc:713 artGetFramesPerSecond()` | minor | fixed |
| FA2 | **`getAnimDistance` used wrong direction anchor, causing walk snap-back.** RE-FIXED 2026-06-03 — the 2026-06-02 "fix" switched both anchors to direction 0 (NE), but NE has oblique/negative x displacement that makes `(total+16)/32` give 0 or negative → clamped to 1 hex/cycle. For 2-hex-per-cycle animations (e.g. player walk, dir1.lastOx=69), shift accumulated 2-hex worth (~69px) but position advanced only 1 hex (~32px), causing a 37px snap-back on cycle end. Correct anchor: direction E (index 1) whose x displacement is purely horizontal (+32px per hex), so `floor((totalE+16)/32)` gives exact hex count. CE ref: `Art.xOffsets[rotation]` — rotation 1. | `object.ts getAnimDistance` | `art.h Art.xOffsets` | major | fixed |
| FA3 | **`actionFrame` hit-frame sync. FIXED 2026-10-06** — the pipeline export had been fixed once and then lost in the b282cca revert (§30). `tools/frmpixels.py` again stores `actionFrame` per direction in `imageMap.json` (regenerated; all other entries byte-identical). Runtime: `Critter.staticAnimation()` takes an `onActionFrame` callback fired when the frame index reaches that direction's action frame (or just before the end callback); `Combat.attack()` now plays the weapon's ATTACK sound on frame 0 and runs the new `resolveAttack()` (hit roll, damage, impact sound, target reaction) on the action frame, matching CE `actions.cc:598 _action_melee` / `_action_ranged` (`animationRegisterPlaySoundEffect(..., delay = artGetActionFrame(art))`). Verified: an unarmed punch (`hmjmpsaq`, action frame 3) resolves at frame 3, ~325 ms in. | `tools/frmpixels.py`, `src/object/critterAnimation.ts`, `src/combat/Combat.ts` | `art.h ArtFrame.actionFrame`; `actions.cc:598` | major | fixed |
| FA4 | **Combat walk-speed boost wired.** FIXED 2026-06-04 — `Critter.updateWalkAnim` adds `Config.combat.combatSpeed` to walk/run `fps` while `globalState.inCombat`. Player-speedup pref is moot in DH2 so the boost always applies during combat. CE ref: `animation.cc:3287 animationComputeTicksPerFrame`. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` | `animation.cc:3287` | minor | fixed |
| FA5 | **Walk start: `obj.shift={x:0,y:0}` is truthy; frame 0's static ox/oy is skipped.** FIXED 2026-06-02 — renderer condition changed to `obj.shift !== null` so `{x:0,y:0}` correctly falls through to `frameInfo.ox/oy`. CE ref: `object.cc _obj_offset()`. | `renderer.ts:311` | `object.cc _obj_offset()` | low | fixed |
| FA6 | **FID composition / weapon stance animation.** FIXED 2026-07-27 — `canEquip()` always returns true so NPCs equip their inventory weapons (CE ref: `critter.cc critterEquipCurrent`). `Weapon.getAnim()` extended to cover run/shoot/weapon-reload. `getAnimation()` weapon path now checks FRM existence via `globalState.imageInfo` and falls back to unarmed skin 'a' when the armed FRM is absent (CE ref: `art.cc buildFid()`). Full armed-pose visuals require the asset pipeline to extract weapon-armed FRM sets; graceful degradation is now in place for when they are. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts`; `src/critter/Weapon.ts` | `art.cc buildFid()`; `critter.cc critterEquipCurrent`; `proto_types.h ItemWeaponData.animCode` | medium | **FIXED 2026-07-27** |
| FA7 | **FRM art transition caused sprite position jump.** FIXED 2026-06-03 — three sources: (1) directionOffset mismatch between FRMs (e.g. idle y=5 → draw y=0 = 5 px y jump); (2) non-zero per-frame ox at transition frame (idle dir0 frames 2–10 have ox=1; ignoring this = 2 px x jump); (3) frame-width center-anchor change (dir4: idle w=23 → draw w=34 = 6 px anchor shift, total -12 px). Fix: `artOffset: Point` field on `Obj`, updated at every art switch using exact zero-jump formula: `artOffset.x = floor(newW/2)−floor(oldW/2) + oldDirOff.x−newDirOff.x + oldOx[F]−newOx[0]`, `artOffset.y = (newH0−oldHF) + oldDirOff.y−newDirOff.y + oldOy[F]−newOy[0]` (height term added 2026-06-04, see FA12). Computed synchronously before `lazyLoadImage` (imageInfo always available). Resets to 0 on walk end (matches CE's `objectSetLocation` reset). Full model in `wiki/animation.md §4.2`. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` (`Critter.staticAnimation, clearAnim`); `src/renderer.ts` | `animation.cc ~2886 artGetRotationOffsets + artGetFrameOffsets`; `object.cc objectSetLocation` | medium | **FIXED 2026-06-03** |
| FA8 | **Sprite vanishes for one frame when a new animation starts (async texture load gap).** FIXED 2026-06-03 — `staticAnimation` previously assigned `this.art = newArt` before calling `lazyLoadImage`; if the GL texture for `newArt` had not yet loaded, the renderer drew nothing for the duration of the async load. Fix: deferred all art-state changes (`this.art`, `this.frame`, `this.anim`, `this.animCallback`, `this.artOffset`) into the `startAnim` callback so the old art remains visible until the texture is confirmed loaded. For already-cached textures `lazyLoadImage` fires synchronously so there is no visible delay. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` (`Critter.staticAnimation`) | `animation.cc artLoad()` | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-03** |
| FA9 | **Frame 0 of a new animation was shown for 0 ms (skipped).** FIXED 2026-06-03 — `staticAnimation` set `this.lastFrameTime = 0`, causing the fps check `time - 0 >= 125ms` to pass on the very first rAF tick, advancing to frame 1 immediately and skipping frame 0 entirely. Fix: `this.lastFrameTime = window.performance.now()` inside `startAnim` so frame 0 is held for a full fps interval before advancing. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` (`Critter.staticAnimation`) | `animation.cc animationComputeTicksPerFrame()` | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-03** |
| FA10 | **Walk cycle had asymmetric partial boundary — visible limp.** FIXED 2026-06-03 — `updateAnim` used `this.frame === currentPartial.endFrame` as the partial-switch trigger; this fires after the boundary frame is already set, so partial 0 consumed 5 intervals and partial 1 only 3 (5:3 ratio) for an 8-frame/2-partial walk → visible limp. Secondary bug: `nextFrame = startFrame + 1` skipped the first frame of each new partial. Fix: trigger changed to `this.frame + 1 >= currentPartial.endFrame` (pre-boundary) and `nextFrame = partials.actions[partial].startFrame` (no +1), giving symmetric 4:4 intervals. `if (partial === 0) nextFrame = 0` special case removed (now redundant). | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` (`Critter.updateAnim`) | `animation.cc anim_run_sequence() partial-action loop` | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-03** |
| FA11 | **Idle animation cycle had an extra inter-cycle delay.** FIXED 2026-06-03 — `updateLoopingAnim` assigned `this.lastFrameTime = time` during the between-cycle wait, so when the wait expired the fps check `time - lastFrameTime ≈ 0` would not pass until one additional fps interval (~125ms) elapsed, adding a dead pause before each cycle. Fix: `this.lastFrameTime = this.nextIdleAnimTime` during the wait so the first advance fires promptly after `nextIdleAnimTime`. | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` (`Critter.updateLoopingAnim`) | `animation.cc animationComputeTicksPerFrame()` | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-03** |
| FA12 | **Per-orientation pixel drift on weapon draw — three-part fix.** FIXED 2026-06-04 — **Part 1 (mid-idle contamination, 2026-06-03):** `staticAnimation` baked `iOxF` into `artOffset_draw`. Fix: `srcF = oldFrames[0]` when `this.anim === 'idle'`. CE ref: `art.cc artGetFrameOffsets`. **Part 2 (K_cycle accumulation, 2026-06-03):** clearAnim zero-jump with `prev` caused −2px/cycle drift for dir2 of hmjmps (K_cycle ≠ 0). Interim fix: `artOffset = {0,0}` hard reset, bounded drift at the cost of a residual Y snap at settle. **Part 3 (Y height correction + clearAnim formula, 2026-06-04):** both `staticAnimation` and `clearAnim` were missing the `(newF0.h − srcF.h)` height term in the Y formula. Confirmed by log: hmjmpsia h=65 → hmjmpsid h=63 → artOffset.y was 4 instead of 2 → 2 px Y jump on every weapon draw. Final fix: (a) `(newF0.h − srcF.h)` added to both Y formulas; (b) `clearAnim` now uses the full zero-jump formula with current artOffset as `prev` (true zero-jump at draw→idle settle). K_cycle drift bounded by subsequent walk resets (CE `objectSetLocation` semantics). | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts` (`Critter.staticAnimation, clearAnim`) | `art.cc artGetFrameOffsets`; `object.cc objectSetLocation` | minor | **FIXED 2026-06-04** |

| FA13 | **Walk/run loop stutter and jumps at animation changes.** FIXED 2026-10-14.<br>**Walk:** the fixed partials dropped the leftover sub-hex offset at every hex, losing 1–9 px per hex. The run loop was worst.<br>**Art changes:** the FA7/FA12 anchor-pinning `artOffset` is not CE.<br>**Fix:** both now port CE's `obj->x/y` bookkeeping: `walkModel 'ce'` (`_object_move`) and `animOffsetModel 'ce'` (`_object_animate`, `_dude_stand`). Details in `wiki/animation.md` §4.3.<br>The old models stay reachable through the console (`setWalkModel('dh2')`, `setAnimOffsetModel('dh2')`). | `src/object/critterAnimation.ts`; `src/object/Critter.ts`; `src/renderer.ts` | `animation.cc _object_move, _object_animate, _dude_stand`; `object.cc objectGetRect` | medium | **FIXED 2026-10-14** |
| FA14 | **Crash every frame when a critter without walk art moves.** FIXED 2026-10-15. Example: a spore plant (`maplnt`, stand art only) creeping in combat. `walkTo` set a missing walk FRM, and `updateAnim` then threw on `imageInfo[art].fps`. Now `walkTo` returns false when the move FRM does not exist, as CE `_object_move` does nothing without art. The AI back-off step now checks that return value. | `src/object/Critter.ts`; `src/combat/Combat.ts` | `animation.cc _object_move` (artLock) | major | **FIXED 2026-10-15** |

Both features now coexist independently: hold Spacebar to sweep-highlight everything (DH2 QoL, unaffected by the preference), or leave the `item_highlight` preference on for CE-accurate hover-only highlighting. | `src/input.ts`, `src/globalState.ts`, `src/renderer.ts`, `src/ui_options/preferences.ts` | `game_mouse.cc:680`; `game_mouse.cc:1949 gameMouseLoadItemHighlight()`; `preferences.cc`; `settings.h:33 item_highlight` | minor | fixed |
| CI13 | **Outline "fill" look (CI11) made configurable with fill/border alpha — kept by user request rather than chasing a true 1px-only edge.** FIXED 2026-06-18, corrected by CI14 — user observed that CI11's 4-offset-stamp technique reads as a solid fill (red for hostiles, yellow for items) rather than a thin border, since a 1px screen-space shift barely changes which pixels fall inside a sprite's silhouette, so the 4 stamps nearly fully overlap the sprite's own footprint. User liked the fill look and asked to keep it, but wanted alpha control over the fill vs. an additional border layer. Added `u_outlineAlpha` shader uniform (the existing binary `texel.a > 0.5 ? 1.0 : 0.0` became `texel.a > 0.5 ? u_outlineAlpha : 0.0`), tunable live via `setOutlineFillAlpha()`/`setOutlineBorderAlpha()`. Not CE-accurate (CE draws true single-pixel edges with no fill, see CI11) — a deliberate, requested stylistic departure, not a bug. **The first implementation of this (stacking border-then-fill as two direct draws) turned out not to give independent control at all — see CI14.** | `shaders/fragment.glsl`, `src/render/webglContext.ts`, `src/render/webglDraw.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/main.ts` | n/a — DH2-specific, not a CE behavior | low | fixed |
| CI14 | **CI13's fill/border alpha sliders weren't actually independent — `setOutlineFillAlpha()` had no visible effect whenever `outlineBorderAlpha` was at its default of 1.0.** FIXED 2026-06-18 — user reported the fill alpha "seems to have no effect." Root cause: CI13 drew the border stamps first, then drew the fill stamp directly on top of them, both in the *same* outline color. Standard alpha-over-alpha compositing of two same-colored layers reduces to `outlineColor·[1-(1-fillAlpha)(1-borderAlpha)] + litSprite·[(1-fillAlpha)(1-borderAlpha)]` — once `borderAlpha=1` (the default), the `(1-borderAlpha)` factor is `0`, which zeroes out the entire `(1-fillAlpha)(1-borderAlpha)` term regardless of `fillAlpha`'s value. Blending a color over an already-fully-opaque layer of the *same* color is a no-op at any alpha — there was no bug in the alpha math itself, the two-layers-stacked-directly design was just mathematically incapable of giving independent control once one layer is opaque. Fixed by inserting a punch-out step between the two layers: (1) draw the 4 border stamps at `borderAlpha`, (2) redraw the object *normally* (regular lit shader path, not outline mode) at its unshifted position — this restores the interior to the normal sprite, leaving only the border stamps' protruding 1px sliver visible, turning step 1 from "near-total fill" into an actual border — (3) draw one more outline-mode stamp at `fillAlpha` on top of the now-restored normal sprite. Now `borderAlpha=1, fillAlpha=0` gives a crisp opaque border with a normal-colored interior; `borderAlpha=0, fillAlpha=0.5` gives a translucent fill with no border; both can be tuned independently as originally intended. Either alpha set to 0 skips that layer's draw calls entirely (cheap no-op, not just invisible). | `src/render/webglDraw.ts` | n/a — DH2-specific, not a CE behavior | low | fixed |
| CI15 | **Neutral (non-hostile, non-allied) critters added to the outline system in a distinct color — DH2 addition, no CE equivalent.** ADDED 2026-06-18 — user request: be able to spot an unprovoked target (e.g. a neutral critter blocked by a wall) before attacking. CE's outline system (CI11) only ever covers `this.combatants` — anyone enrolled in the active `Combat` instance (player, trigger-team members, or already-hostile critters); a neutral critter elsewhere on the map (not on the player's team, not hostile, not enrolled) was never touched by `refreshHighlights()` and so was never outlined no matter the preference setting. New `'blue'` outline color (`OUTLINE_COLORS.blue = [0.3, 0.6, 1]` in `webglDraw.ts`); benefits from the same post-roof late pass as combat outlines (CI11), so a neutral critter behind a wall is visible the same way a hostile one is. Also reset the default `Config.ui.outlineBorderAlpha` 1.0→0.5 and `outlineFillAlpha` 0.5→0.2 per user request (still tunable via `setOutlineFillAlpha()`/`setOutlineBorderAlpha()`). **First implementation had a gap, caught immediately**: it kept the existing combatant-vs-neutral split — looping `this.combatants` for red/green (skipping anyone not yet hostile and not same-team) plus a second loop over `globalState.gMap.getObjects()` for blue (skipping anyone already in `this.combatants`). User reported starting combat via the HUD gun button gave *no* highlights at all, not even blue. Root cause: `Combat.start()` for a player-initiated fight (no `forceTurn`) already sweeps *every team on the current map* into `triggerTeams` so any enemy can join in — meaning `this.combatants` ends up containing nearly every living critter on the elevation, hostile or not. Those swept-in-but-not-yet-hostile, different-team critters fell into a gap: skipped by the red/green loop (not hostile yet) AND skipped by the blue loop (already counted as "a combatant") — no outline at all. Fixed by dropping the combatant/non-combatant split entirely: `refreshHighlights()` now classifies every live, visible critter on `globalState.gMap.getObjects()` (current elevation) in one pass — hostile → red, same team → green, else → blue — with no reference to `this.combatants` at all. `clearNeutralOutlines()` (map-wide scan clearing any `outline === 'blue'`, called from `Combat.end()`/`forceEnd()`) is kept as a safety net for the same reason it was originally needed: `this.combatants` doesn't necessarily cover every critter that could have been colored blue (e.g. an NPC-initiated fight via `forceTurn` only sweeps the player's and the attacker's team into `triggerTeams`, leaving third-party critters elsewhere uninvolved in `this.combatants` but still blue-outlined). | `src/combat/Combat.ts`, `src/render/webglDraw.ts`, `src/config.ts` | n/a — DH2-specific, not a CE behavior | low | fixed |
| CI16 | **NPC combat-outline fill visible over player character.** FIXED 2026-08-25 — `renderOutlinePass()` runs post-roof (CE ref: `object.cc:874 _obj_render_post_roof()`), so NPC silhouette fills were painted over everything including the player. `refreshHighlights()` already excluded the player (`isPlayer` guard at line 1444), so the player had no outline of their own — but NPCs behind the player in world-space had their fills drawn on top of the player sprite by the post-roof pass. Fix: after `renderOutlinePass()` in `renderer.ts:draw()`, re-stamp the player sprite unconditionally when `globalState.inCombat` to ensure the player always wins painter's order over any outline fill. CE never faced this because it has no fill — only a true 1px edge from a palette-cycling shader, not a stamped silhouette fill. | `src/renderer.ts` | n/a — consequence of DH2's fill-based CI13/CI14 outline system | minor | **FIXED 2026-08-25** |
| CI17 | **All NPCs appeared in armed pose by default regardless of hostility.** FIXED 2026-08-25 — Root cause: DH2 loaded inventory items from maps but never checked the `OBJECT_IN_LEFT_HAND` (0x1000000) / `OBJECT_IN_RIGHT_HAND` (0x2000000) flags that Fallout 2's editor stamps on equipped items, so all inventory weapons ended up equipping by first-in-inventory heuristic (which always picked the weapon). CE has no `critterEquipCurrent()` — it instead scans inventory for these flags via `critterGetItem1()`/`critterGetItem2()` (`inventory.cc:2771/2793`). `fomap.py` already exports `flags` for every inventory item (`fomap.py:348`). Fix: `Critter.init()` and deserialization now scan inventory for the flag pair, placing the matching `WeaponObj` in `leftHand`/`rightHand` and defaulting unmatched slots to `Critter.makeFist()`. Guards whose weapons have `0x2000008` flag appear armed; shopkeepers whose weapons have only `0x8` (OBJECT_FLAT, no hand slot) appear with fists. `equipFromInventory()` (first-weapon heuristic) is kept as a fallback for hostile NPCs in save-games where no flagged weapon is found. When an NPC first turns hostile in combat, `equipFromInventory()` is called to draw their best available weapon (CE ref: combat.cc — critters draw weapons when they enter the combat loop). Hover tooltip shows equipped weapon name and armed/unarmed status (`ui_hud.ts uiShowCombatHover()`). | `src/object/Critter.ts`, `src/combat/Combat.ts`, `src/ui_hud.ts` | `inventory.cc:2771 critterGetItem2()`; `inventory.cc:2793 critterGetItem1()`; `obj_types.h OBJECT_IN_LEFT_HAND/OBJECT_IN_RIGHT_HAND`; `fomap.py:348` | minor | **FIXED 2026-08-25** |
| CI18 | **Combat taunts, combat looks, brightness and mouse sensitivity did nothing. FIXED 2026-10-07** — the preferences screen kept them in local variables, so they were never persisted or applied. They're now `Config.ui` fields, saved to localStorage and read from fallout2.cfg. Taunts gate AI combat messages (`combat_ai.cc:3308`). Combat looks examines what the attack cursor points at (`game_mouse.cc:745`). Brightness applies CE's gamma table (`color.cc colorSetBrightness`: out = clamp(i^b, 0, 63)) as an SVG component-transfer filter on the game canvas. Mouse sensitivity is stored only, since the browser owns pointer speed. The attack-cursor hover now shows only CE's to-hit readout ("NN%" or " X ", team colours from `_colorTable`), not an invented name/HP/weapon panel. | `src/config.ts`, `src/ui_options*.ts`, `src/ui_hud.ts`, `src/gameTick.ts` | `preferences.cc`; `game_mouse.cc:745-777` | minor | fixed |

Switchable via `Config.engine.lightPropagationMode` (`'dh2'` default / `'derived'`) and `setLightPropagationMode()`; compare live with `lightingDebug()`. Does not close LD2/LD-#7-equivalent gaps — it's an alternative algorithm, not a fix, and is known to diverge from `'dh2'` near walls (no wall-facing-direction partial-block nuance — see §14.5). | `src/lightmap/lightDerived.ts`, `src/lightmap.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/main.ts` | n/a — DH2-original, not CE-derived | n/a | added |
| LD9 | **New switchable `'naive'` light-propagation mode (distance-only baseline).** ADDED 2026-06-30 — `src/lightmap/lightNaive.ts` implements `obj_adjust_light_naive()`, a pure hex-distance falloff mode with **zero occlusion** (no wall/object blocking checks at all, light passes through everything). Added as a third comparison option alongside `'dh2'`/`'derived'` to make the occlusion-vs-no-occlusion trade-off visible side-by-side via `lightingDebug()` (now 3-way) — see `wiki/lighting.md §14.7`. Deliberately not a candidate default; expect light to bleed through walls whenever active, by design. | `src/lightmap/lightNaive.ts`, `src/lightmap.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/main.ts` | n/a — comparison baseline, not CE-derived | n/a | added |
| LD11 | **Wall light-occlusion read the wrong flag field → W-E walls bled light (alternating lit/dark stripes along the face).** FIXED 2026-07-02 — the `'dh2'` propagation pass selects one of four CE occlusion rules per wall by its orientation class. CE reads `proto->wall.extendedFlags` (`object.cc:4556`); DH2 read `curObj.pro.flags` (the common PRO *header* flags), where the orientation bits `0x8000000/0x10000000/0x20000000/0x40000000` are absent — so **every** wall fell through to CE's default (no-orientation-bit) branch. That default is correct for the NE-SW/default family (why NW-SE walls looked clean) but wrong for W-E walls, whose occlusion boundary then zig-zagged across the hex stagger → stripes. Fix: read `curObj.pro.extra?.extendedFlags ?? 0` (the field `tools/proto.py readWall` populates and the egg occlusion already uses). The four branch conditions already matched CE exactly (`rotation`/`index` verified against `object.cc:4556-4581`), so this is a one-field change; default/NW-SE walls are byte-identical (no regression), W-E walls now pick the correct branch. Not the shader — `hex-lerp` sampling (RD17/§7) was already correct; this is purely propagation data. `wiki/extended_flags.md §4` had already flagged that CE's light switch uses `extendedFlags`. NOTE: `'derived'`/`'naive'` propagation modes do their own occlusion and are unaffected; CE's non-wall opaque-object occlusion branch (`object.cc:4583`) remains commented out in DH2 (`lightmap.ts`) — separate gap, not this bug. | `src/lightmap.ts` | `object.cc:4552-4581 _obj_adjust_light`; `proto_types.h WallProto.extendedFlags` | major | fixed |
| LD10 | **Object sprites used full per-fragment world-position tile intensity.** FIXED 2026-07-01 (parity-offset corrected 2026-07-02, see RD17) — New uniform `highp float u_objectBaseY` in `fragment.glsl` (default −1 = per-fragment fallback). For object sprite draws, `renderObject()` sets it to the world-Y of the object's tile (via inverse hex formula: `12·ty + 8.4 + 6·tx`); `getWorldTileLight()` uses this fixed Y while still varying world-X per-fragment. Fixes: (1) tall sprites no longer have dark upper pixels — Y is anchored to the tile, not to wherever the fragment lands vertically; (2) light no longer bleeds through walls — with fixed Y, the texture is only sampled along one horizontal strip at the tile level, so bilinear blending never reaches across a wall boundary into the shadow side. `u_tileIntensity` filter stays `gl.LINEAR` (smooth per-hex wash on the floor). Three selectable modes via `setObjectLightingMode()` console command: `'tile-y'` (default, inverse hex formula), `'foot-y'` (bottom of bounding box), `'off'` (full per-fragment — switches texture to `NEAREST` per-draw to suppress bleed, restores `LINEAR` after). `Config.engine.objectLightingMode` controls the live mode. See `wiki/lighting.md §14.9` for the full derivation and mode table. | `shaders/fragment.glsl`, `src/render/webglContext.ts`, `src/render/webglDraw.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/main.ts` | `object.cc:835` | major | fixed |

Only the connected building section the player stands in is hidden; other buildings remain visible. The old `init.ts` `playerMoved → showRoof=false` global handler was removed. DH2's `setRoofLighting()` (`src/render/webglDraw.ts`) passes `GameTime.getAmbientLightNormalized()` as `u_ambient`; `roofDummyTexture` (zeroed 1×1) on tex unit 5 makes the result `max(0, ambient) = ambient`.
